### Reporting Period
* **Start Date:** 2024-12-27
* **End Date:** 2025-12-26

---

### Narrative Summary and Analysis
During the reporting period from 2024-12-27 to 2025-12-26, a total of 1,024 individual case safety reports (ICSRs) were observed in association with exposure to Bisoprolol. Of the total cases, 1,023 were classified as serious, and 1 was classified as non-serious. 

Analysis of demographic data indicates the following distribution across age groups:
* **75+:** 407 cases
* **65–74:** 266 cases
* **45–64:** 204 cases
* **Unknown:** 87 cases
* **18–44:** 44 cases
* **<18:** 16 cases

Regarding sex distribution, reports involved 503 females, 493 males, and 28 cases where sex was reported as unknown. 

Geographically, cases were reported across multiple regions, led by the EU (325 cases) and the United Kingdom (278 cases), followed by France (187 cases), Canada (55 cases), Italy (52 cases), Germany (39 cases), Spain (26 cases), Poland (21 cases), Portugal (9 cases), Belgium (4 cases), United States (4 cases), Ireland [IE] (3 cases), Romania [RO] (2 cases), Sweden (2 cases), Saudi Arabia [SA] (2 cases), Croatia [HR] (2 cases), Ukraine [UA] (2 cases), Finland (1 case), Australia (1 case), Indonesia (1 case), China (1 case), alongside 7 cases with unknown country information.

Reporter qualifications were distributed as follows: 493 reports from physicians, 255 from pharmacists, 161 from other health professionals, and 115 from consumers or non-health professionals.

---

### Summary Analysis of Cases
A total of 1,024 cases were recorded in the dataset. Case seriousness is heavily skewed, with 1,023 serious cases and 1 non-serious case identified. 

Evaluation of reported outcomes (totaling multiple outcomes across cases) shows:
* **Recovered/resolved:** 1,324
* **Unknown:** 1,130
* **Not recovered/not resolved/ongoing:** 545
* **Recovering/resolving:** 420
* **Fatal:** 137
* **Recovered/resolved with sequelae:** 34

Additionally, data quality analysis identified 6 reaction-outcome mismatch rows within the dataset.

---

### Reaction / Adverse Event Analysis
The most frequently observed adverse reactions reported in association with Bisoprolol exposure across all cases are as follows:
1. **Acute kidney injury:** 22 reports
2. **Drug ineffective:** 12 reports
3. **Cerebral haemorrhage:** 7 reports
4. **Hyponatraemia:** 6 reports
5. **Cholestasis:** 6 reports
6. **Hypokalaemia:** 6 reports
7. **Hepatic cytolysis:** 5 reports
8. **Drug interaction:** 5 reports
9. **Joint swelling:** 5 reports
10. **Muscle spasms:** 4 reports
11. **Pemphigoid:** 4 reports
12. **Cardiogenic shock:** 4 reports
13. **Atrioventricular block:** 4 reports
14. **Hypotension:** 4 reports
15. **Pneumonitis:** 4 reports

The distribution of top serious reactions mirrors the overall top reactions identically, with "Acute kidney injury" leading at 22 serious reports down to "Pneumonitis" at 4 serious reports.

---

### Serious Cases / 15-Day Alerts
Within the dataset, alert analysis parameters indicate:
* **Alert cases:** 1,023
* **Alert serious cases:** 1,023
* **Alert non-serious cases:** 0

All serious cases observed in the dataset met the criteria for alert tracking as configured in the evidence packet.

---

### Trends and Important Observations
Monthly case reporting volumes over the reporting period show the following distribution:
* **2024-12:** 21 cases
* **2025-01:** 75 cases
* **2025-02:** 94 cases
* **2025-03:** 83 cases
* **2025-04:** 78 cases
* **2025-05:** 80 cases
* **2025-06:** 84 cases

* **2025-08:** 64 cases
* **2025-09:** 76 cases
* **2025-10:** 102 cases
* **2025-11:** 75 cases
* **2025-12:** 83 cases

Reporting volume fluctuated throughout the period, with the highest single-month case counts observed in July 2025 (109 cases) and October 2025 (102 cases).

### History of Safety-Related Actions
*This information was not supplied in the evidence packet; therefore, a history of safety-related regulatory actions is unavailable for this report.*

---

### Limitations
* Reference safety information (such as company core data sheets or product labeling) was not supplied; consequently, expectedness evaluations could not be performed.
* MedDRA System Organ Class (SOC) classifications were not provided in the evidence packet.
* Data quality checks noted 6 reaction-outcome mismatch rows within the underlying dataset.
* Causality assessment between Bisoprolol and the reported adverse events cannot be established from the supplied ICSR counts alone.
